([rassim-medkour/spine](https://github.com/rassim-medkour/spine/tree/HEAD/cursor/skills/record-contract) · ★7 · 2 copies)
- **record-contract**: Use when adding language-neutral contract cases for a route or behaviour and recording the expected output from the original service, including side effects (DB, cache, storage, queues, logs). ([bulubuloa/claude-service-port](https://github.com/bulubuloa/claude-service-port/tree/HEAD/skills/record-contract))
- **record-contracts**: Activate when the user asks to document component-to-component protocol contracts, event type mappings, API specifications between services, message channel schemas, or mentions 契约、接口协议、事件映射、消息通道、API契约、协议对齐、message mapping、schema contract. ([vicat47/vicat-home-bus](https://github.com/vicat47/vicat-home-bus/tree/HEAD/.opencode/skills/record-contracts))
- **record-conventions**: Convention over configuration for a repository's experiment records: enumerate by naming, commits beat the manifest, generated indexes regenerated in the same commit as their evidence, pinned counts, one decision row per landed unit, dated worklog names beside stable living names. Use when finding what a programme has run, or adding a record to one; not when the task is to trust the manifest, or to hand-write a page a script generates. ([emliunix/home.conf](https://github.com/emliunix/home.conf/tree/HEAD/skills/record-conventions))
- **record-correction**: Turn a correction from the developer into a permanent rule change so the same mistake does not come back, and write down Mollie or Shopware behaviour a maintainer explained. Use whenever the developer rejects, corrects or overrides something, and whenever a maintainer explains how Mollie or Shopware really behaves. Trigger words — "no, do it this way", "that is wrong", "not like that", "I already told you", "nein, so nicht", "das ist falsch", "hab ich schon gesagt", "merk dir das", "schreib das auf", "das macht Mollie so". - **record-correction**: Turns Brian's correction of how something was built into a playbook rule with an example, gets his yes, and writes it to the right tier. Use whenever Brian corrects the team's code, style, structure or behavior, or says 'remember this' or 'always/never do X'.
